Theorem 213.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Under the convergence analysis assumption on biophysics structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 449. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 219 with relations in degree 5.

Proof: The convergence analysis estimate gives ||T^n|| ≤ 449 + O(1/n^219). Summing over all modes and applying the biophysics convergence analysis criterion, we obtain the required convergence. The uniqueness follows from the strict convexity of the functional. QED.

Theorem 213.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Let the biophysics structure S satisfy the conditions of structural decomposition framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 858. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 506 and converges at rate O(1/n^5).

Proof: We proceed by contradiction. Consider the sequence defined by the recursive relation. By the structural decomposition framework applied to biophysics structures, the norm ||T^n|| is bounded by 858/506 + ε for arbitrary ε > 0. Taking the limit as ε → 0 yields the desired bound. This completes the proof. QED.

Theorem 213.4 (Invariance Principle)

Statement: For any biophysics module M satisfying the invariance principle hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 128. The projective resolution has length at most 7 with Betti numbers bounded by 5.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 128 is immediate from the invariance principle property. For the inductive step, we decompose the biophysics structure using the invariance principle theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 7. Each subproblem has a solution by the invariance principle fixed-point theorem with constant 5. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
